# PART XIV — MODULAR RETURN OPTICS AND THE GALACTIC WELL: THE VERSION-19 BRANCH [T/V/D/S/O]

*This part is deliberately ambitious and deliberately provisional. It asks whether the full-sky antipodal family of the galactic arena, broken by secondary structure into a countable set of stable recurrent photon-history classes, could be rendered as finite galaxy images with a unitary multiplicative redshift $1+z = e^\eta$ — the same $\eta$ giving the observed time dilation and an apparent Hubble law — and whether the Milky Way's own well could set the scale of that dilation. Nothing here is established. Parts 0–XIII are unchanged by it; standard quantum mechanics is retained; the Jacobi gate of §XIII.10 is the opening condition; the full secondary-inclusive optical metric is used throughout; and every mechanism begins as [S/O] unless it is actually derived. The branch's job is to find out whether the idea can be made coherent while keeping the empirical meaning of astronomical redshift — and to fail cleanly if it cannot.*

## XIV.0 The ambition and its firewall [D]

**The hypothesis.** What an observer calls an external galaxy may be a rendered, recurrent photon-history object of the single galactic arena — a two-path history returning through the twin structure — rather than a second gravitational arena at a literal cosmological distance.

**The non-negotiable target.** The branch must reproduce an apparent Hubble law in *actually observed* redshift: one multiplicative shift of every spectral line, $(1+z)$ time dilation of transients, the measured behaviour of angular-diameter and luminosity distances, Tolman-like surface-brightness dimming, and ordinary foreground lensing. A relation that merely correlates image size or interference phase with an invented distance is not enough.

**The firewall inherited from §XIII.10.** Canonical position cannot be visual position for returning light: the Sun's own light would return at the solar constant. So a rendered return must be registered at whatever flux the observable map assigns — smaller than naive return by a factor of order $(D_{\rm app}/\ell)^2$, $10^7$ to $10^{11}$, *conditionally*, since that presupposes $D_{\rm app}$ acting as a luminosity distance, which the branch has not yet derived — and the observer's own return must lie in the readout kernel by an invariant, not by fiat. The brightness budget of §XIV.8 needs no such assumption. That is the price of admission, paid before any return map is built.

> **Three layers, never merged [D].** *Canonical refocusing* — where a returning family converges on the arena — is not *visual image location* — where the readout places it — and neither is *source identity*. The branch's claim is not that the Sun's photons circle the arena and one return is called Andromeda. It is that global conjugate geometry creates recurrent history freedom; collective secondary structure breaks and selects it; stable recurrent classes result; and the readout assigns sky position, morphology, flux and $z$. The self-return test is then a tool rather than a verdict: **what invariant distinguishes a recurrent class that renders as an external galaxy from the self-return class that must lie in the readout kernel?** Found without "do not render the Sun" put in by hand, it would be real progress; not found, the objection returns with full force.

## XIV.1 The round antipodal family, and what breaks it [T/V]

On the round $S^3(\ell)$ the first conjugate traversal, $L_1 = \pi\ell$, is reached by a whole two-parameter family of great circles, the critical manifold $\mathcal C_1\simeq S^2$, on which the optical action is constant — the self-image problem in its sharpest form (§XIII.10). The branch's first mechanism is standard perturbation theory of a Morse–Bott critical manifold: turn on the actual arena perturbation, $g_{\rm opt} = g_{\rm round}+\epsilon\,\delta g$, and restrict the action to the family,

$$S_{\rm eff}(\Omega) = S_0+\epsilon V(\Omega)+O(\epsilon^2)$$

whose stationary members, $\nabla_\Omega V(\Omega_\ast) = 0$, are the candidates for isolated recurrent histories. The secondaries do not open the family — the round geometry does that — they carve it.

**Computed [V].** With $V(\Omega) = 2GM\int ds/r(s)$ along each great circle, the first-order optical length of a weak secondary: one secondary leaves a *degenerate ring* of minima, by the axial symmetry of a single point mass about the source–antipode axis; several break the family into isolated members, **about two per secondary** — a sharp maximum for the circle passing through the mass, and a saddle between. That is a property of the sum's singular structure: each star's $1/r$ diverges on the ray through it whatever the other stars do, and the saddles follow by the Euler characteristic — with one hygiene condition: the $1/r$ perturbation is singular on the ray through each point mass, so the count was made with every secondary softened to a smooth potential of finite core, $1/\sqrt{r^2+\epsilon^2}$, on which compact Morse theory applies; strict point masses would need the singular directions excised and the boundary accounted for, and the conclusion that the degeneracy breaks does not depend on it. It is **not a galaxy count** — galaxy counts themselves run from $10^{11}$ to $10^{12}$ depending on which faint systems are included — and the maxima are rays through the stars, absorbed rather than imaged. The right object is the return map's periodic orbits, below; the first-order count shows only that the degeneracy does break.

## XIV.2 The return map and modular classes [S/O]

Homotopy cannot supply a catalogue: $\pi_1(S^3) = 0$ and $\pi_1(\mathbb{RP}^3) = \mathbb Z_2$. The candidate object is a return map on the canonical photon-history state $\xi = (\Omega, J, D_\lambda J, \text{phase data}, \mu_{\rm opt},\ldots)$,

$$\mathscr P:\mathcal X_{\rm ph}\to\mathcal X_{\rm ph},\qquad \mathscr P^n(\xi)\sim\xi$$

where "one traversal" — source to twin, twin to source class, one projective half-circumference, one pass through the correspondence — is itself open, and $\sim$ means equality after exactly the identifications the readout legitimately forgets. For fixed period the recurrent set ${\rm Fix}_n(\mathscr P)$ is at most countable *if* the perturbed map is nondegenerate — hyperbolicity, twist, transversality, broken continuous symmetry — which must be shown, not assumed. An optional second integer, the phase closure $\Delta\Phi = 2\pi m$ of two legs, would give labels $(n,m)$. The stability matrix $M_n = D\mathscr P^n|_{\xi_\ast}$ classifies members as elliptic or hyperbolic and locates image *islands*: a finite apparent image would be the sky projection of a stable island or caustic patch, an extent achromatic to leading geometric-optics order — finite-source and wave effects can add wavelength dependence — rather than a stationary-phase width, which would carry a wavelength dependence real galaxies do not show. A meridian coordinate $\mu_{\rm opt}$ could enter as a morphology variable only through $\mu_{\rm opt}\mapsto M_n\mapsto$ caustic and shear class $\mapsto$ rendered morphology, never by declaration; it is not the charge rapidity $\mu$ of §X.0 unless derived to be.

## XIV.3 Apparent distance [S/O]

Gravitational separation is bounded, $0\le d_{\rm grav}\lesssim\pi\ell$; recurrent data are not: $n$, $m$, cumulative phase, holonomy and action. An apparent distance $D_{\rm app} = \mathcal D(n,m,\mu_{\rm opt},\Phi_{\rm hol},\Delta T,M_n,\ldots)$ built from them need not equal geodesic distance — the intended route to a compact gravitational arena with effectively noncompact optical distance. It must be universal, never tuned object by object.

## XIV.4 Two no-gos that shape the redshift [T/V]

**The static wall.** Identical source and twin wells have identical lapse, so ordinary gravitational redshift between them is zero (Part IV); path delay changes phase, enclosed area changes holonomy, and two-path interference makes frequency *fringes*, $\Delta\phi(\omega) = \omega\Delta T$ — none is a universal multiplicative shift of every line.

**The coboundary.** If a per-return increment were a difference of one scalar potential, $\rho(\xi) = f(\mathscr P\xi)-f(\xi)$, then on a period-$n$ orbit $\eta_n = f(\mathscr P^n\xi)-f(\xi) = 0$. In particular a dilation connection $\mathcal A_z = d\ln N$ gives $\oint d\ln N = 0$ on every closed orbit. So a nonzero closed-orbit redshift needs a non-exact structure: a genuine holonomy, a readout dilation not reducible to endpoint differences, or a larger clock structure. The galactic well may *source* a dilation law; it cannot enter as an endpoint potential difference.

## XIV.5 The spectral-dilation readout [T/S]

The least invasive candidate leaves quantum composition unchanged and puts redshift in the canonical-to-observed readout. With $\eta = \ln(1+z)$ the dilation

$$(U_\eta\psi)(\omega) = e^{\eta/2}\,\psi(e^\eta\omega)$$

is unitary on $L^2(\mathbb R_+,d\omega)$ **[T]** and sends a packet at $\omega_{\rm em}$ to $\omega_{\rm obs} = e^{-\eta}\omega_{\rm em}$, every feature shifted by the same factor, while its time-domain conjugate stretches transients by $e^\eta = 1+z$ — the requirement that tired-light mechanisms fail, and that a survey of 1,504 Type Ia supernovae meets with a time-dilation exponent of $1.003\pm0.005\pm0.010$. That is a design success, not yet a physical one: $\eta$ has still to be derived from the well and return geometry, and flux, $D_L$, $D_A$, surface brightness and lensing must come from the same map. Three readings of $\eta$ are kept apart and tried in order: a clock normalization between canonical and detector frequencies, in which no photon loses energy in transit; a larger unitary sector with which energy is exchanged; a modified optical Hamiltonian. Redshift is composed by $\eta_n(\xi) = \sum_{j<n}\rho(\mathscr P^j\xi)$, additive in $\eta$ and multiplicative in $1+z$; the dilation is noncompact, $\mathbb R_+$, and must not be identified with the compact $U(1)$ phase without derivation. A dilation connection with $\eta = \oint\mathcal A_z$ was the last resort, after existing canonical structures had been tried; version 20 adopts a specific one by decision — the relative connection of a Weyl pair whose mean is the metric geometry — and derives its scale character (Part XV).

## XIV.6 The galactic well as the dilation scale [V/D/S/O]

**The coincidence.** Dividing $M_{\rm gal}\sim cH_0\ell^2/G$ by $c^2\ell/G$ gives its conceptual form,

$$\frac{GM_{\rm gal} }{c^2\ell}\;\sim\;\frac{H_0\ell}{c}$$

the dimensionless depth of a galactic well on the arena scale against the dimensionless Hubble redshift of one arena radius. The speculative bridge: the number that measures the Galaxy's arena-scale gravitational strength may set the dilation accumulated by one recurrent traversal, with the Milky Way's well the universal dial and the individual recurrent state the position on the relation. In a first-traversal calibration, $D_{ {\rm app},1} = q_D\ell$, a well functional $\mathcal W = C_W\,GM_{\rm grav}/c^2\ell$ and $\eta_1 = \alpha\mathcal W$ give $M_{\rm gal} = \beta\,cH_{\rm app}\ell^2/G$ with $\beta = q_D/\alpha C_W$; the heuristic $q_D = \pi$, $\alpha = 1$, $C_W = 4\pi$ gives $\beta = 1/4$ — to be derived or rejected, never inserted. The arithmetic, with $M_{\rm gal} = 6\times10^{10}M_\odot$ and $H_0 = 70$ km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$ **[V]**:

| arena radius $\ell$ | $cH_0\ell^2/G$ | $\beta_{\rm MW} = GM_{\rm gal}/cH_0\ell^2$ | where this radius comes from |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| 7.0 kpc | $2.4\times10^{11}M_\odot$ | $1/4$ | the heuristic coefficient |
| 8 kpc | $3.1\times10^{11}M_\odot$ | $1/5$ | the Sun's Galactocentric distance, the addendum's proposal |
| 13 kpc | $8.3\times10^{11}M_\odot$ | $1/14$ | the isotropic profile's 220 km/s (§IV.8) |
| 16 kpc | $1.3\times10^{12}M_\odot$ | $1/21$ | the compact-source 220 km/s |
| 25 kpc | $3.1\times10^{12}M_\odot$ | $1/51$ | the detached wing's arena law, $\beta_{\rm BTFR}\approx1/49$ |

Since $\beta\propto\ell^{-2}$, "the coefficient" encodes the arena radius: a derived $\beta$ would be a radius-selection rule, $\ell = \sqrt{\Gamma GM_{\rm gal}/cH_{\rm app} }$ with $\Gamma = 1/\beta$. That is why the addendum's $\ell\approx8$ kpc is a **version-19 selection hypothesis**, not a result of Part IV, which puts the plateau at 13–16 kpc.

**The literal well, computed [V].** The addendum's functional is the hemisphere flux $\mathcal W_{\mathcal H} = \ell^{-1}\int_{\partial\mathcal H}*_h\,d\ln N$ through the equator separating the source's hemisphere from its twin's, evaluated on the arena's actual static solution — source, same-sign twin and compensating closure background, with $\ln N\simeq-2\,\delta\phi$ in the weak field. It **vanishes identically**: the lifted source is even under the antipodal map, so its potential is even about the equator and its normal derivative there is zero, for the compact source and for the Milky Way's profile alike. Gauss on the closed slice says the same — the source's hemisphere holds the source and exactly minus one half of the background, and the curvature term $3\int\delta\phi$ that remains is killed by the same symmetry. The addendum's first null test, "the hemisphere flux vanishes by symmetry", is met, and its most literal option — a source-centred hemisphere on the cover — fails. The natural alternative, the arena-scale well *depth* $2[\delta\phi({\rm centre})-\delta\phi({\rm equator})]$, is finite but **dominated by the galaxy's own central self-potential** $M/4h$: in units of $GM_{\rm grav}/c^2\ell$ it is $1.4$, $2.6$ and $3.3$ for the Milky Way's profile at $\ell = 8$, $13$ and $16$ kpc, and $26$ for a compact lump, with the twin and the background adding only $\pm0.3$. A universal Hubble calibration cannot be hypersensitive to baryonic packing, so this is not the dial either. What survives is a surface the branch has not yet defined: a history-dependent surface spanned by the two recurrent legs, or a meridian-selected region [O].

**The history-dependent functional, computed [T/V/D].** Three candidates were proposed for the surviving object — a Jacobi-weighted tidal integral along the pair, the curvature flux through the pair's ribbon, and the boost component of the Lorentz holonomy around the two-leg loop — and the arena decides among them.

*The boost holonomy.* In any static spacetime the curvature has no boost component on spatial surfaces: every $R^0{}_{ijk}$ vanishes, checked on isotropic Schwarzschild, so around a purely spatial loop the Lorentz holonomy is a pure rotation **[T/V]**. The two photon legs are null, and for their loop the boost part reduces at first order to the tidal integral along the ray contracted with the separation, $\eta^i\sim\int E^i{}_jJ^j\,ds$: the elegant candidate collapses onto the cheap one, and the static arena supplies no $SO(1,1)$ of its own. A derived rapidity would have to come from non-static structure — the fiber, the meridian, or time — all open.

*The Jacobi-weighted tidal integral,* $\mathcal W_J = \ell^2\int E_{JJ}|J|^2ds\big/\!\int|J|^2ds$, on the isotropic Milky Way profile: with the source at the Galaxy's centre it is sign-definite and isotropic, but only $0.04$–$0.07$ of the needed $H_0\ell/c$ — the log-enhanced $f$; from the Sun's position its magnitude swings twenty-fold with direction and its sign flips with the pair's orientation, because the tidal tensor is trace-free. It cannot give a redshift that is all one sign **[V]**.

*The null focusing column.* What the sign analysis leaves is the trace: the Ricci focusing a photon accumulates along its history, made affine-invariant with the conserved Killing energy of the static arena — $\mathcal E = -\xi\cdot k$, $\bar k = k/\mathcal E$, $d\bar\lambda = \mathcal E\,d\lambda$, which stays affine, unlike a normalization by local static observers, whose energy varies with the lapse — and taken relative to the exact round arena of the same $\ell$, $\delta R_{ab} = R_{ab}-R^{(0)}_{ab}$:

$$\mathcal F[\gamma] = \ell\int_\gamma\delta R_{ab}\,\bar k^a\bar k^b\,d\bar\lambda,\qquad \mathcal F[\Xi] = \tfrac12\big(\mathcal F[\gamma_+]+\mathcal F[\gamma_-]\big)$$

For comoving dust $\delta R_{ab}\bar k^a\bar k^b\,d\bar\lambda = (8\pi G\,\delta\rho/c^2)\,ds/N$, from which $\Lambda$ drops out; the lapse correction is of order $10^{-6}$, and the numbers below are unchanged. The subtraction is essential to the definition: the round background alone focuses by $\ell\int_0^{\pi\ell}2/\ell^2\,ds = 2\pi$ per traversal, a million times the Hubble number, and that common focusing is what makes the conjugate gate; the Galaxy's *departure* from it is the small, class-dependent quantity. This **renormalized, history-averaged Ricci focusing functional** is exactly the kind of quantity the coboundary no-go allows — a path integral of a density, not an endpoint difference — additive traversal by traversal, symmetric under leg exchange, zero wherever the geometry equals the round reference — which is not the same as physical vacuum — and attached to Jacobi-open classes by construction. For a source at the Galaxy's centre it gives $1.05$, $1.53$ and $1.63$ times $H_0\ell/c$ at $\ell = 8$, $13$ and $16$ kpc, and analytically $GM\ell/c^2h^2$, so that

$$\frac{\text{focusing column per arena radius} }{H_0\ell/c} = \frac{GM_{\rm gal} }{cH_0h^2} = 1.8$$

**independent of $\ell$** **[V]**. The coincidence becomes $M_{\rm gal}/h^2\approx2\,cH_0/G$ — $18.5$ against $10.2$ kg m$^{-2}$ — the Freeman-limit surface-density coincidence the detached wing already carries (§XII.1), no longer tied to the arena radius. The two jobs previously forced onto $\ell$ separate: **$\ell$ sets the recurrence geometry — topology, conjugacy, return periods — while the Galaxy's baryonic column, $GM/h^2$, sets the candidate dilation per traversal**; no choice among 7, 8, 13 or 16 kpc is needed for the coincidence to hold. That is the best-behaved candidate found. **Its limitation is the off-centre observer, and it is not a small one:** from a source at the Sun's radius the column runs from $-0.7$ to $+3.4$ times the target with direction, negative whenever a ray misses the dense centre and sees only the compensating underdensity, and its direction-average falls from $1.1$–$1.6$ at the centre to about zero at $8$–$12$ kpc **[V]**. So it can serve only if the rendered class is a *collective* object whose column is the Galaxy's own, centred — the brief's picture of a class, but an assumption about what a class is, not a result. Three things stay postulated here: that class, the identification $\eta = C\mathcal F$ with $C$ of order one-half to one, and $D_{\rm app}$ itself **[S/O]** — and §XIV.9 derives the first two further from the optical transfer matrix.

**Local suppression [D].** The Sun and the Earth generate no Hubble-like readout: $\eta$ attaches only to Jacobi-open recurrent classes, and within the solar system the gate stays shut (§XIII.10). Local secondaries bend and focus rays; they do not open the global sector, and they do not set $H_{\rm app}$.

**The Tully–Fisher fork [D].** The detached wing's arena law $GM = A_{\rm dyn}\ell^2$ gives $v^4 = 4C_{\rm plat}^2A_{\rm dyn}GM$, slope four for any constant coefficient; a well-Hubble law would give $A_{\rm dyn} = \beta cH_{\rm app}$ *if* one $M$–$\ell$ relation governed both dynamics and optics. The wing's normalization wants $\beta\approx1/49$; the heuristic well argument wants $1/4$; the document's own plateau radii sit at $1/14$ to $1/21$. Branch A, one universal coefficient, must explain a factor of order ten or fail; Branch B, linked but distinct coefficients $A_{\rm dyn}$ and $A_z$ with a derived ratio, is safer, and is the one carried.

**Emergent $G$ [O].** The rearrangement $G = \beta\,cH_{\rm app}\ell^2/M_{\rm inertial}$ is dimensional only: if $G$ entered the well that set $H_{\rm app}$, solving backward is circular. A genuine route needs an operational inertial mass, an $H_{\rm app}$ from readout geometry, an independently selected $\ell$ and a derived $\beta$, and then $G_{\rm pred}$ must reproduce the weak-field law, the equivalence of masses, the plateau, the local Schwarzschild comparison and Law I's coupling.

## XIV.7 The apparent Hubble law and the cosmological slot [S/O]

At low redshift $z\simeq\eta\simeq(H_{\rm app}/c)D_{\rm app}$, so the branch needs $\eta/D_{\rm app}$ constant across the rendered population — a strong, falsifiable requirement — and the full curve $z(D_{\rm app})$ must match the observed distance-redshift relation, not merely its slope. The status of $H_{\rm app}$ must be stated: derived from return-map statistics, from $\ell$ and a large dimensionless recurrence number, or from a canonical coupling — or simply inserted, in which case the claim that the model has no cosmological slot is weakened, and this document says so rather than hiding it.

## XIV.8 Celestial-sphere acceptance tests [D]

| observable | what the branch must reproduce or explicitly generalize |
|:--|:--|
| spectral lines | one multiplicative $1+z$ for all lines of one source |
| transients | $\Delta t_{\rm obs} = (1+z)\Delta t_{\rm em}$ from the *same* $\eta$ |
| distances | the measured $D_A(z)$ and $D_L(z)$, and duality $D_L = (1+z)^2D_A$ unless a tested premise is replaced |
| surface brightness | Tolman-like dimming before source evolution |
| lensing | foreground lensing tied to foreground mass; lensed images sharing one spectrum and $z$; normal shear, magnification and delays; recurrent multiplicity distinct from ordinary multiple imaging |
| achromaticity | a nearly achromatic rescaling, no interference comb masquerading as redshift |
| high $z$ | the whole Hubble diagram, and $T_{\rm CMB}(z) = T_0(1+z)$ if the claim ever becomes universal |
| source diversity | different morphologies, populations, metallicities, activity and variability from recurrent states of one arena — a major open burden |
| independent variability | transients in different apparent galaxies must not be correlated copies; a weaker ontology, in which each apparent galaxy is a distinct canonical history class rather than a literal duplicate, is kept separate from the strong one |
| clustering | large-scale statistical structure, if the branch claims the whole extragalactic sky |
| brightness budget | in matching units the Galaxy's integrated starlight, $200$–$2{,}000$ nW m$^{-2}$ sr$^{-1}$ at optical wavelengths, is $20$ to $180$ times the measured optical background, $11.2\pm1.7$ nW m$^{-2}$ sr$^{-1}$ at $0.6\,\mu$m and itself mostly integrated galaxy light: the readout must send all but a few per cent of returned starlight into its kernel, and the self-return requirement of §XIV.0 must hold |
| redshift comb | $\eta_n = n\eta_1$ with $D_{\rm app}\propto n$ too rigidly would put galaxies on a discrete redshift comb, which is not observed; the island invariant, a further modular index, or the map to $D_{\rm app}$ must supply continuous variation |
| recurrence-age bound | with $\eta_1\simeq0.4\,H_0\ell/c$ per traversal and $\pi\ell/c$ per traversal, a redshift $z$ needs $t_{\rm can}\simeq\pi\ln(1+z)/0.4H_0$ of canonical propagation, and $\ell$ cancels: $10$ Gyr at $z = 0.1$, $76$ Gyr at $z = 1$, doubled for full returns, against the Galaxy's age of about $13$ Gyr. So large $z$ cannot come mainly from traversal number, or the recurrent object is not a literal chronological history, or the simple cumulative branch fails |
| finite-width / high-redshift test (20.6) | since exactly twin-paired returns do not accumulate $\eta$ (§XV.3), large observed redshift must arise *within a single admissible class*, through $y_0$, the mixed-curvature profile or nonlinear finite-width effects; the local calibration $\eta\sim H_0\ell/c\sim4\times10^{-6}$ sits $2.4\times10^4$ below $z = 0.1$ ($\eta = 0.095$) and $1.7\times10^5$ below $z = 1$ ($\eta = 0.693$); the present formula is a small-$\epsilon$ variation and finite existence with lumps is open, so high $z$ cannot be extrapolated from it. Determine the finite-$\epsilon$ scale character, and whether the allowed class family spans $\eta = \ln(1+z)$ from $O(10^{-6})$ to $O(1)$ without violating the complex-lift construction — failure is fatal to a universal high-$z$ reading |
| the images' age | two times, not one: the twin at $\pi\ell/c$ and the self-return at $2\pi\ell/c$ — $0.8$ and $1.6\times10^5$ years at $\ell = 8$ kpc, $1.6$ and $3.3\times10^5$ at $16$ — so transients in recurrent images are Galactic events of those epochs |

> **The brightness budget, sourced [V].** The extragalactic side is the New Horizons direct measurement of the optical background, $11.16\pm1.65$ nW m$^{-2}$ sr$^{-1}$ near $0.6\,\mu$m, most of it accounted for by integrated galaxy light. The Milky Way side is the *integrated starlight* of standard night-sky tabulations, quoted in $S_{10}$ units — one tenth-magnitude star per square degree, or $27.78$ mag arcsec$^{-2}$ — at roughly $30$–$100\,S_{10}$ at high Galactic latitude and several hundred toward the plane, in the $V$ band near $0.55\,\mu$m. The conversion uses the Vega zero point, $f_\lambda\simeq3.63\times10^{-11}$ W m$^{-2}$ nm$^{-1}$ at $V = 0$, and $\nu I_\nu = \lambda I_\lambda$ at $550$ nm: one $S_{10}$ is then $6.6$ nW m$^{-2}$ sr$^{-1}$, so a $22$ mag arcsec$^{-2}$ dark sky is $1{,}350$, and the integrated starlight is $200$–$2{,}000$ nW m$^{-2}$ sr$^{-1}$ — $20$ to $180$ times the optical background. Two caveats cut the other way: the bandpasses differ slightly ($0.55$ against $0.61\,\mu$m), and the starlight we receive directly is dust-extinguished through the disk, whereas a return travelling through the barren hemisphere would not be, so the returned flux would if anything be larger. The conclusion does not depend on any distance interpretation: whatever the readout assigns, all but a few per cent of returned starlight must go into its kernel.

Foreground lensing is both a constraint and a laboratory: the branch may use the same conjugate threshold ordinary lensing uses, but must say what survives once ordinary lensing is subtracted.

## XIV.9 Relative optical monodromy and the collective focusing class [T/V/D/S/O]

*Rather than postulate $\eta = C\mathcal F$, derive as much of it as possible from the Jacobi system that already defines the gate and the return map.*

**The transfer matrix [T].** On the two-dimensional screen the optical tidal matrix splits into trace and traceless parts, $\mathcal T = \tfrac12R_{ab}k^ak^b\,I+\mathcal T_{\rm shear}$ — the Sachs decomposition into isotropic focusing and Weyl shear. The full screen system is four-dimensional, $Y = (J^1,J^2,\ell DJ^1,\ell DJ^2)$, and its transfer map is symplectic, $\mathsf M\in Sp(4,\mathbb R)$. What follows first is its **isotropic $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ sector** — the identical scalar block obtained when the trace part of $\mathcal T$ is isolated — and the full symplectic object comes after. In the variables $(J,\ell J')$ that sector is a first-order system $y' = \mathsf A(s)y$; one traversal gives a transfer matrix $\mathsf M_\Xi$, and the round arena gives the reference $\mathsf M_0$, a rotation by $s/\ell$, equal to $-I$ after one traversal. The **relative monodromy** $\mathsf R_\Xi = \mathsf M_0^{-1}\mathsf M_\Xi$ removes the round background geometrically rather than by subtracting a number, and equals $I$ for the control arena. To first order in the isotropic perturbation $\delta\mathcal R = \tfrac12\delta R_{ab}k^ak^b$, with $\sigma = s/\ell$,

$$\log\mathsf R_\Xi = \ell\int_0^{\pi\ell}\delta\mathcal R(s)\begin{pmatrix}\sin\sigma\cos\sigma & \sin^2\sigma\\ -\cos^2\sigma & -\sin\sigma\cos\sigma\end{pmatrix}ds$$

an element of $\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb R)$, which decomposes into a rotation and a squeeze,

$$\theta_\Xi = \tfrac12\,\ell\!\int\!\delta\mathcal R\,ds = \tfrac14\mathcal F[\Xi],\qquad \eta_\Xi = \tfrac12\,\ell\,\Big|\int\delta\mathcal R\,e^{2is/\ell}\,ds\Big|$$

with the squeeze axis at the phase of the second Fourier harmonic — the centroid angle $2s_0/\ell$ only for a localized or symmetric perturbation.

**What this gives [V].** Checked against direct integration of the transfer system, to three digits:

| | rotation $\theta_\Xi$, compact | squeeze $\eta_\Xi$, noncompact |
|:--|:--|:--|
| the uniform closure background, or any uniform perturbation | nonzero: the conjugate-point shift | **zero**: its second harmonic over a traversal vanishes |
| a compact lump anywhere along the traversal | $\mathcal F_{\rm lump}/4$ | $\mathcal F_{\rm lump}/4$, wherever it sits |
| the Milky Way profile, class centred on the Galaxy | $\mathcal F/4$ | $0.38$–$0.43$ times $H_0\ell/c$ at $\ell = 8$–$16$ kpc |
| from the Sun's radius, toward the centre / sideways / away | can be negative | $0.65$–$0.82$ / $0.04$–$0.05$ / $0.02$ times $H_0\ell/c$: positive throughout |

So the trace sector of the relative monodromy *already contains a noncompact parameter*, derived from the optical transfer matrix rather than added as a connection, and three things come with it. The normalization is derived, not fitted: for a sharply localized lump $\eta = \mathcal F/4$, the $\tfrac14$ from the Sachs $\tfrac12$ and the half-period of a traversal; for an extended profile the value is the second-harmonic-weighted one in the table, not $\mathcal F/4$. This $\tfrac14$ has nothing to do with the old heuristic $\beta = 1/4$ of the $M\sim\beta cH_0\ell^2/G$ relation. The background subtraction is automatic: uniform density rotates the beam's phase and never squeezes it, so the compensating underdensity that made the raw column negative for rays missing the Galaxy drops out. And the squeeze is one-signed as a *magnitude* — but a positive squeeze is not yet a positive redshift: an $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ squeeze has reciprocal branches $e^{\pm\eta}$, and the geometry has supplied a magnitude and an axis, not which branch the detector reads as the spectral dilation. That choice must come from time orientation, endpoint orientation, or the canonical readout — and it, rather than another well functional, is now the real redshift bridge. *(Version 20 dissolves it structurally: the candidate dilation is carried by a scale character, which has one scale factor, and the squeeze keeps the job of shape, §XV.2.)* And the two Jacobi sectors divide the labour the phenomenology wants: **trace, with its rotation and squeeze, for the gate and a scalar dilation; traceless shear for shape, morphology and splitting.**

**What it does not yet give [D/O].** A Sachs squeeze dilates the beam's phase space, not the photon's frequency; the step from isotropic optical dilation to the spectral and temporal readout of §XIV.5 is still the postulate. Squeezes about different axes do not add as scalars, since $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ is non-abelian — though a periodic class repeats the same generator, so that $\eta_n = n\eta_1+O(\delta\mathcal R^2)$ within one class — the first-order generator adds, while the finite squeeze of a product that also contains rotation need not — which is what "modular" can now mean. And **the off-centre observer remains decisive**: individual rays from the Sun's radius still span a factor of forty between the directions toward and away from the centre. The scalar must be an invariant of a *class*, not of a ray.

**The full symplectic monodromy [T/V].** The round arena supplies a natural scale between $J$ and $\ell DJ$, hence a positive metric on Jacobi phase space; with its adjoint $\dagger_0$ the object $\mathsf C_\Xi = \mathsf R_\Xi^{\dagger_0}\mathsf R_\Xi$ has, by symplecticity, stretch eigenvalues in reciprocal pairs $e^{\pm2\chi_1}$, $e^{\pm2\chi_2}$, and

$$\eta_{\rm iso} = \tfrac12(\chi_1+\chi_2),\qquad \gamma_{\rm aniso} = \tfrac12(\chi_1-\chi_2)$$

are invariant under rotations of the Sachs screen and divide the labour exactly as the phenomenology wants: $\eta_{\rm iso}$ for a scalar dilation, $\gamma_{\rm aniso}$ for shear and morphology. Computed on the isotropic Milky Way with its Weyl screen shear $\tfrac32GM(<r)\,r_\perp^2/c^2r^5$ **[V]**: the transfer map is symplectic to seven digits; for the centred class $\gamma_{\rm aniso}$ vanishes and $\eta_{\rm iso}$ equals the isotropic-sector squeeze exactly; for rays from the Sun toward or away from the centre the shear vanishes by symmetry and the same holds; for oblique rays the two blocks' second harmonics combine with a relative phase, so that $\eta_{\rm iso}$ and $\gamma_{\rm aniso}$ are the symmetric and antisymmetric combinations of the block squeezes — of the same size as the isotropic and shear harmonics, but not equal to them separately.

**The collective class, defined [D].** An arbitrary island contains rays the observer never reads. Since the Photon History Postulate fixes observed endpoints, take an *observer-conditioned* return section $\mathcal S_O$, an island $\mathcal I_O\subset\mathcal S_O$, and the Liouville measure $\mu_L$ that the Poincaré map preserves, and set

$$\bar\eta_{\mathcal I_O} = \frac{1}{\mu_L(\mathcal I_O)}\int_{\mathcal I_O}\eta_{\rm iso}(\xi)\,d\mu_L$$

An apparent galaxy is already meant to be the sky projection of an island, so its redshift being an island invariant is far less arbitrary than assigning it to one ray. The crucial prediction is then testable before anything else: **$\bar\eta_{\mathcal I_O} > 0$ and narrowly distributed across islands, for an observer at $R_0\neq0$** — averaging over rays the observer actually reads, not over rays it never sees. If island averaging recentres the directional swing, this candidate lives; if the swing survives, it fails on the record.

**The prize, and a caution [S/O].** If one transfer object carries everything, Part XIV collapses from five readout assignments to one: trace → $\eta$, $z$ and perhaps $D_L/D_A$; traceless part → shear and morphology; return period → $D_{\rm app}$; island size → angular extent; the trivial-monodromy class → the self-return kernel. And the caution goes straight into the acceptance tests: with $\eta_n = n\eta_1$ and $D_{\rm app}\propto n$ too rigidly, galaxies would sit on a discrete redshift comb, which they do not; the island invariant, a further modular index, or the map to $D_{\rm app}$ must supply enough continuous variation to wash it out.

**The program.** Build one return map for the smoothed Milky Way profile with a controlled ensemble of lumps; find its recurrent classes, islands and stability matrices; compute $\bar\eta_{\mathcal I}$; test its sign and spread for $R_0 = 8$ kpc; only then ask whether the relative monodromy fixes the readout coefficient, and whether repeated traversals give $\eta_n = n\eta_1$ with $D_{\rm app}\sim nD_\ast$, from which the low-$z$ Hubble law would follow with no further input.

## XIV.10 Status and falsification ledger [D]

| grade | contents |
|:--|:--|
| **standard mathematics and physics** | Morse–Bott perturbation theory; Poincaré return maps, periodic points and stability matrices; the static-wall and coboundary no-gos; the unitary dilation on $L^2(\mathbb R_+)$; cosmological distance measures; the supernova time-dilation measurement; Etherington reciprocity; Tolman dimming; the CMB temperature–redshift relation |
| **verified here** | one secondary leaves a degenerate ring; several carve about two first-order stationary members each, which is not a galaxy count; the source-centred hemisphere flux vanishes by symmetry; the arena-scale depth is packing-dominated; the coincidence's arithmetic and the radii it encodes; returned starlight would exceed the optical background twenty- to two-hundredfold; the boost part of the two-leg holonomy vanishes for spatial loops in any static spacetime and reduces to the tidal integral for the null loop; the tidal functional is sign-indefinite off centre; the null focusing column gives $1.0$–$1.6$ times $H_0\ell/c$ from the centre, independently of $\ell$, and about zero on average from the Sun's radius; the relative monodromy of the screen Jacobi system decomposes into a rotation $\mathcal F/4$ and a squeeze $\tfrac12\ell\lvert\int\delta\mathcal R\,e^{2is/\ell}ds\rvert$, to which uniform density contributes nothing and a compact lump contributes $\mathcal F/4$ wherever it sits; from the Sun's radius the squeeze is positive throughout, $0.02$–$0.8$ of the Hubble number; the full $Sp(4,\mathbb R)$ relative monodromy separates $\eta_{\rm iso}$ from $\gamma_{\rm aniso}$, the shear vanishing for the centred class; the recurrence-age bound, $\ell$ cancelling |
| **derived here** | canonical position cannot be visual position for returning light, so a rendered return must be registered at a flux smaller than naive return — by a factor of order $(D_{\rm app}/\ell)^2$ if $D_{\rm app}$ acts as a luminosity distance, a conditional constraint on the observable map; local suppression through the Jacobi gate; a constant $\beta$ preserves the Tully–Fisher slope; the Tully–Fisher fork |
| **proposals** | the Modular Galaxy Readout — an apparent galaxy as a stable recurrent class with $\mathscr P^n\xi\sim\xi$; the Apparent Hubble Readout — $1+z = e^{\eta}$ with the same $\eta$ dilating time; the galactic well as the dilation scale, through a surface not yet defined; the null focusing column as the dilation cocycle, $\eta = C\rho(\Xi)$, for collective classes centred on the Galaxy; the island-averaged squeeze $\bar\eta_{\mathcal I}$ of the relative monodromy as the class redshift, with its coefficient $1/4$ derived |
| **failed so far** | the source-centred hemisphere flux as the dial; the arena-scale depth as a universal dial; $\ell\approx8$ kpc as anything but a selection hypothesis; the boost holonomy as an $SO(1,1)$ source in the static arena; the tidal functional as a one-signed redshift; the simple cumulative reading of large $z$ as traversal number, which the Galaxy's age caps at $z\lesssim0.1$ |
| **open** | the definition of one traversal; nondegeneracy of the return map; stable islands and achromatic image extents; the flux-redistribution formalism; a non-exact dilation cocycle and where its scale comes from; the surface through which the well would act; source diversity; whether $H_{\rm app}$ is derived or inserted; and above all an invariant that places the self-return class in the readout kernel; whether a collective class's column is universal — whether the return map's islands centre on the Galaxy rather than on individual sources — and where the constant $C$ comes from; whether island averaging recentres the directional swing; the step from beam dilation to spectral readout; the redshift comb; which squeeze branch the detector reads, the redshift bridge; $\eta_n = n\eta_1$ beyond first order; where large $z$ comes from if not from $n$ |

> **The single question [D].** Can one static galactic projective arena produce a discrete celestial population of finite apparent galaxies whose spectra, time scales, angular sizes, fluxes and lensing reproduce the empirical redshift–distance sky, including an apparent Hubble law, through the dynamics and readout of recurrent Jacobi-open photon-history classes — without modifying the Born rule, and without hiding a Friedmann cosmology inside a free observable map? If yes, the branch has found a real extension. If no, it should fail cleanly, on this ledger. **The next calculation is fixed by the ledger.** A very good additive history scalar has been found — the renormalized Ricci focusing functional, non-exact, additive and one-signed for centred classes, with the Hubble magnitude to within a factor of $1$–$1.6$ independently of the arena radius — though not yet shown to be a cocycle of the return map in the dynamical sense; and §XIV.9 derives its noncompact partner, the squeeze of the relative monodromy, from the optical transfer matrix itself, with coefficient $1/4$ and no background to subtract. What remains is one construction — the return map's islands, and whether their average squeeze is positive and narrowly distributed for an observer at $8$ kpc — with two tests to pass first: the recurrence-age bound, which already caps the cumulative reading at $z\lesssim0.1$, and the choice of squeeze branch that would make a dilation a redshift.
