# PART XV — THE BI-CONNECTION SUBSTRATE AND THE UNIVERSAL NULL-HISTORY PRINCIPLE: THE VERSION-20 BRANCH [T/V/D/S/O]

*Two of the author's intuitions open this part, and one of them is adopted by decision rather than derived: that the middle geometry should be the symmetric resolution of two deeper transports — a pair of Weyl-type connections sharing one null geometry — with candidate dilation carried by the scale character of their relative transport. What follows states the principle, verifies what the pair does, answers the one question that decides its relation to Part XIV, and records what remains. Parts 0–XIV are unchanged.*

## XV.0 The decision and the principle

**The intuitions, in the author's terms.** Since a galaxy's image is already a two-history object for light, uniqueness of that galaxy under *both* light and gravitational waves demands an exactly analogous two-history structure for gravitational radiation — with no mechanism that could privilege one, "as if being able to determine which one is more objective." And rather than add readouts after the fact, "bake this straight into spacetime": let the middle space itself emerge as a combination of two deeper structures, designed so that the dual gravitational-wave picture lives on it and is wired to the electromagnetic one by the same no-privileging principle — a substrate-deepening move that might make the Hubble behaviour emergent rather than imposed.

> **The foundation, independent of any model [T].** What the branch rests on is a theorem, not the Jacobi machinery built on it. A round three-sphere of radius $\ell$ is flat to order $(d/\ell)^2$: rays from a point fan out as in flat space, so between two nearby points the ray is unique and isolated. Positive curvature bends the fanning rays back, and at distance $\pi\ell$ every ray from a point reconverges at its antipode — the twin — so the two endpoints are joined by a continuum of paths, sharing both ends and spreading apart by up to order $\ell$ between them. The transition is a threshold, the conjugate point, not a drift. It is robust: by Bonnet–Myers, any closed space whose Ricci curvature is at least the round sphere's forces a conjugate point on every geodesic longer than $\pi\ell$ — given that lower bound — and for this particular arena the explicit weak-lump calculation, which shifts and splits the focus by parts per million without removing it (§XIII.10), is the stronger evidence. Lumps can add paths locally, through ordinary gravitational lensing — standard physics, not the arena's mechanism, which is why the solar focus lies at $548$ AU while nothing in the solar system joins two points by more than one path. **Aligned when zoomed in; separable at the arena scale.** The Jacobi equation is only the calculator; the Photon History Postulate and the monodromy squeeze are optional, and this part uses the fact alone: in §XV.3 the imaginary displacement of a class is a Jacobi field vanishing at the real endpoints, so the local half is $Y = 0$ with no scale character, and the global half is the sine profile at the twin.

**The Universal Null-History Principle [S].** The existence and canonical equivalence of histories is decided *before* the propagating field's spin is specified:

$$\mathcal N_{\rm can}\subset\mathbb P(T\mathcal M)\ \text{transverse to}\ \ker dq,\qquad d\iota(\mathcal N_{\rm EM}) = \mathcal N_{\rm can}\big|_{B_{\rm rep} },\qquad dq(\mathcal N_{\rm can}) = \mathcal N_{\rm GW}$$

— one canonical null structure on the middle space, of which the electromagnetic cone on $B_{\rm rep}$ is the restriction and the gravitational-wave cone on $B_{\rm grav}$ the image, a commuting diagram rather than an equality between objects on different manifolds; the Jacobi gate $\mathcal J_{\rm can}$ and the history class $\Xi_{\rm can}$ live on $\mathcal M$ likewise, read on $B_{\rm rep}$ for light and on $B_{\rm grav}$ for gravitational waves — while spin one and spin two are representation data, $\rho_1(\Xi_{\rm can})\neq\rho_2(\Xi_{\rm can})$, with different polarizations, amplitudes and couplings. The standard foothold is real **[T]**: at leading geometric-optics order both Maxwell waves and high-frequency gravitational waves propagate on the null geodesics of the background, with amplitude and polarization transported along them, and both lens, interfere and show wave-optical behaviour at caustics. The principle is therefore exact at leading eikonal order only; beyond it, gravitational waves acquire known polarization-dependent corrections, and the theory must not claim more.

## XV.1 The bi-connection [T/V]

On the middle-space geometry, with its conformal null structure $[h]$, take two exchange-related connections

$$\nabla^\pm_a h_{bc} = \mp2A_ah_{bc},\qquad \Gamma^{\pm a}{}_{bc} = \Gamma^a{}_{bc}\pm\big(\delta^a_bA_c+\delta^a_cA_b-h_{bc}A^a\big)$$

with the exchange $+\leftrightarrow-$ sending $A\to-A$. Their mean is the Levi-Civita connection, $\tfrac12(\nabla^++\nabla^-) = \nabla^{\rm LC}$, and their difference $K = \tfrac12(\nabla^+-\nabla^-)$ carries the relative structure **[V]**. Three facts, each checked symbolically: the difference tensor contracted on a null vector is along that vector, $C^a{}_{bc}k^bk^c = 2(A\cdot k)k^a$, so all three connections have the same unparametrized null geodesics **[V]**; along a $\nabla^\pm$-parallel field the squared norm $h(v,v)$ changes as $e^{\mp2\int A}$, lengths as $e^{\mp\int A}$ **[V]**; and everything that couples to $\bar\nabla = \nabla^{\rm LC}$ sees the metric geometry of Parts 0–IX unchanged.

**Where it lives — the correction of version 20.2.** As a pair of connections *on the real middle space* this structure cannot carry the redshift, because §XV.7 shows that the scale part of the canonical complex connection vanishes on every real direction: on the real locus all three connections coincide. What survives is better. The pair is the shadow of *one* $\mathbb C^\times$-connection on the complexification, seen from a complex history and its $\rho_E$-conjugate: by equivariance, transporting along the conjugate history with the one connection equals transporting along the original with the conjugate connection — the offloading identity of §XV.6 realized by $\rho_E$ itself. Real single histories see only $U(1)_H$ and the metric, which is the firewall, now derived rather than stipulated; pairs of conjugate lifts see the difference.

## XV.2 The relative transport and its scale character [V/D]

**Definitions.** Reserve *relative transport* for the full element: for two connections carried around the same loop, $H_{\rm rel}(\Xi) := {\rm Hol}_{\nabla^-}(C_\Xi)^{-1}{\rm Hol}_{\nabla^+}(C_\Xi)$, or, in the complex form of §XV.7, one connection carried around the loop $\widetilde\gamma_+\cup\overline{\widetilde\gamma_-}$ of conjugate lifts. On the full screen system of §XIV.9 this element lies in the conformal symplectic group,

$$CSp(4,\mathbb R)\simeq\mathbb R_+\cdot Sp(4,\mathbb R):\qquad \text{the scale part supplies the overall homothety; the Jacobi system supplies focusing, shear, squeeze and rotation}$$

— on one scalar block $GL^+(2,\mathbb R)\simeq\mathbb R_+\times SL(2,\mathbb R)$. What is unambiguously abelian is its *scale character*, $\chi_{\rm sc}(H_{\rm rel}) = e^{-\eta_\Xi}$: for the Weyl pair around one real loop, $\eta_\Xi = 2\oint_{C_\Xi}A = 2\int_{\Sigma_\Xi}F_A$ (the factor two from comparing the two connections rather than either with the mean); for one connection around the conjugate-lift loop, $\eta_\Xi = \int_{\Sigma_{\mathbb C} }F_A$ over the complex ribbon. Both are Stokes-expressible, non-exact, history-dependent and additive; the branchwise composition — each connection on its own leg — is a sum rather than a loop integral, exchange-odd, and not a cocycle, which is why the closed-time-path dictionary of §XV.6 must double transports around one loop, not histories with separate connections.

**What it is not [V].** The scale character does not reproduce the Jacobi squeeze of §XIV.9 and cannot: the Weyl transport has determinant $e^{4\int A}\neq1$ on a screen block, while the Jacobi monodromy is symplectic with determinant one. The two are complementary — scale and shape — and the reciprocal-branch problem of §XIV.9 dissolves, since a homothety has one scale factor.

**The real loop carries nothing [D].** With $A|_{TE} = 0$ (§XV.7), the pull-back of $A$ to the real locus vanishes and so does $i^*F_A = d(i^*A)$: every ribbon tangent to the real geometry has zero scale flux, and the redshift candidate of versions 20 and 20.1 — evaluated on the real Jacobi ribbon — is zero. The scale character of a class is evaluated instead on the complex ribbon between the conjugate canonical lifts of its legs, $\widetilde\gamma_\pm = \gamma_\pm\pm iY$, which is where §XV.3 goes. The Jacobi geometry decides when a real class exists; the complex geometry decides its scale.

## XV.3 Where the flux lives [V/D/O]

**The complex ribbon and the Hopf flux, at first order [V/D].** Type the objects downstairs: the Hopf curvature is basic, $F_H = \pi_H^*\bar F_H$ with $\bar F_H$ a 2-form on $\mathcal M$, and $k,Y\in T\mathcal M$ are evaluated on their horizontal lifts. Let a real leg $\gamma$ have a one-parameter family of complex lifts $\widetilde\gamma_\epsilon = \gamma+i\epsilon Y+O(\epsilon^2)$ with $\rho_E\widetilde\gamma_\epsilon = \widetilde\gamma_{-\epsilon}$, and let $\Sigma_{\mathbb C}$ be the complex ribbon between $\widetilde\gamma_{\pm\epsilon}$. By the bridge of §XV.7, $F_A(k,JY) = -F_H(k,Y)$ on the real locus and $F_H(k,JY)$ vanishes there, so the conjugate lifts' phases cancel and their scale character is

$$\eta_\Xi(\epsilon) = 2\epsilon\int_\gamma\bar F_H(Y,k)\,ds+O(\epsilon^3)$$

— odd in $\epsilon$, because exchanging the lifts reverses the relative scale. The formula is exact only on the real locus: a finite ribbon samples the continued curvature away from it. On the Hopf model the residual scales as $\epsilon^3$, with coefficient $0.119$ in the test geometry, and the phase cancels to $10^{-17}$ **[V]**. **At first order the scale character of a class is a Hopf flux** — the flux of a curvature the theory already has, through the real ribbon that the imaginary displacement sweeps along the ray. No new independent field enters; what enters is the full Hopf connection.

**The imaginary displacement is a Jacobi field, and the gate returns [V/D].** A complex geodesic variation of a real null geodesic has, as its first-order term, a complex Jacobi field, since the Jacobi equation is linear over $\mathbb C$; so $Y = \tfrac1i\,\partial_\epsilon\widetilde\gamma_\epsilon|_0$ is a real Jacobi field along the ray. A real source and a real observer force $Y(0) = Y(S) = 0$, and a Jacobi field vanishing at both ends exists only when they are conjugate: **conjugacy is the necessary infinitesimal gate for a nontrivial complex family.** So the gate of §XIII.10 returns as the existence condition of a complex lift: zoomed in, $Y = 0$ and no scale character; at the twin, $Y = y_0\sin(s/\ell)\,\hat e$. In the round arena the finite family exists explicitly: on the complexified sphere, $\cos(s)\,p+\sin(s)(d_R+id_I)$ with $|d_R|^2-|d_I|^2 = 1$ and $d_R\cdot d_I = 0$ is a complex geodesic from $p$ to $-p$ for every such $d$, with imaginary displacement $\sin(s)\,d_I$ — the sine profile, exactly **[V]**; once lumps split the degeneracy, finite existence is a nonlinear theorem not yet proved. This is the foundation of §XV.0 doing its work twice: the local half as $Y = 0$, the global half as the sine profile.

**The decisive component, the parity law and the sign [V/D/O].** The formula is linear in $\hat e$: a free imaginary direction would give classes in opposite-sign pairs. Part XV's original candidate for the canonical direction is the imaginary *meridian* displacement of Part X, along the fourth spatial direction of $\mathcal M$ off the representational base — on the round $S^4$ the unit normal $N$ of the base, parallel along its geodesics, so that $Y = y_0\sin(s/\ell)\,N$ is a legitimate Jacobi field. That candidate is excluded by §XVI.8 if the actual Hopf data are plane-reflection equivariant, and the admissible displacement is reopened as the representation problem of §XVI.9; what follows is stated for it as originally proposed. What the class then needs is the mixed curvature, which is gauge-invariant where the meridian component of the connection is not, so it is the fundamental observable:

$$f_\Xi(s) := \bar F_H(N,k),\qquad \eta_\Xi = 2\epsilon y_0\int_0^{\pi\ell}\sin(s/\ell)\,f_\Xi(s)\,ds$$

Since $\sin(s/\ell)>0$ between source and twin, **the minimal acceptance criterion is the weighted moment, $\int_0^{\pi\ell}\sin(s/\ell)f_\Xi\,ds>0$, for every admissible class**; pointwise positivity, $f_\Xi\ge0$, is the *sufficient local positivity theorem* — a stronger target, since a function may change sign and keep a positive weighted moment. §XV.7 computes the component on the local model: in a meridian-invariant gauge, $f = -k\cdot\nabla a_{\rm mer}$, with $a_{\rm mer}$ the meridian component of the Hopf connection — zero for the product connection, not forced by topology or smoothness, an unsourced deformation of Structure III (item 3 of §XI.2). In that gauge, integrating by parts with $Y$ vanishing at the ends, $\eta_\Xi = (2\epsilon y_0/\ell)\int a_{\rm mer}\cos(s/\ell)\,ds$, and since $\cos((\pi\ell-s)/\ell) = -\cos(s/\ell)$ the parity statement is exact and no more: *a component even between source and twin gives zero; an odd one gives a moment that is symmetry-allowed* — allowed, not guaranteed nonzero, and not guaranteed one-signed, since an odd profile can wiggle and cancel **[T]**.

**The twin-equivariant parity law [D, conditional].** Structure III's opposite Chern signs at the two crossings are a statement about the orientation of $\bar F_H$ on linking spheres, and do not by themselves say how a longitudinal component transforms. What does is the geometry of one meridian crossing the base twice: with the meridian circle oriented, its tangent is $+N$ at the source crossing and $-N$ at the twin crossing, because the circle continues over the pole and comes back through the base the other way. Take the connection to be *twin-equivariant* — invariant under the involution whose invariance of the bundle produces Structure III's signs. Then, since the twin map carries the class through $d$ onto the class through $-d$ reversed, $\tau\gamma_d(s) = \gamma_{-d}(\pi\ell-s)$, the law relates *paired* histories: $f_d(s) = f_{-d}(\pi\ell-s)$ — checked on explicit twin-invariant connections on the round arena to $10^{-10}$, including one with no further symmetry, for which the per-history evenness $f_d(s) = f_d(\pi\ell-s)$ fails by seventy percent **[V]**. Per-history evenness needs, in addition, invariance under $d\to-d$ — a profile axisymmetric about the meridian has it — or a class average invariant under that exchange; and because $\sin(s/\ell)$ is itself symmetric about the midpoint, the weighted moment is already the same for the two paired members, $\eta_d = \eta_{-d}$ **[V]**. The topological parity and the optical parity law thus hang on one assumption. Positivity is separate: if the meridian component decays monotonically away from each crossing, $f = -\partial_ra\ge0$ and $\eta_\Xi>0$; a wiggling profile keeps the parity law and loses the sign **[V]**. Twin equivariance fixes the transformation law, not the sign; the sign is the profile's, and the profile is item 3's. Version 21.4 adds two parity tests from the plane duality (§XVI.8): with reflection-equivariant Hopf data, the meridian displacement gives planar classes no scale character and mirror classes opposite signs — a systematic hemispheric reversal the sky forbids — while the normal displacement survives that test; so the choice of displacement and the parity assignments of item 10 stand ahead of positivity.

**The self-return kernel, and the convention the age bound selects [D, conditional].** With the imaginary direction along the global normal $N$, the round smooth self-return $p\to-p\to p$ — the continuation of the same great circle, whose second half is the twin image of the first, point for point — sees $-f$ on the return leg: it has zero net scale character, and repeated returns alternate $0,\eta,0,\eta$ with no cumulative growth **[V]**. The statement covers the round smooth self-return and any exactly twin-paired continuation; a perturbed recurrent loop through the observer need not be twin-paired point for point, so persistence of the kernel under the perturbed return map is a theorem to prove or a test to run, not a result in hand. Within its scope this is the kernel behaviour the self-return test of §XIV.2 requires of the observer's own class, and it removes the cumulative reading outright. What it establishes is *scale-kernel* membership, not readout-kernel membership: a class with $\eta = 0$ can still carry phase, amplitude, angular structure and flux, and the returned-starlight problem of §XIV.2 needs the self-return's visual observable to vanish, $\mathcal O_{\rm vis}(\Xi_{\rm self}) = 0$, or a unitary routing into a non-visual sector. **Scale kernel is not visual kernel.** The other convention, the imaginary direction along the meridian's own orientation at each class's source, gives $+\eta$ on every leg — cumulative growth, which the recurrence-age bound of §XIV.8 already excludes beyond $z\approx0.1$. So the global-normal convention passes the recurrence-age test where the cumulative meridian-oriented one fails it — the bound rules out that reading, not every alternative — and with the global-normal convention the twin image becomes the *candidate* one-traversal carrier of dilation — whether that half-return is what renders as an external galaxy remains Part XIV's observable-map proposal, given its structure in Part XVI — where §XVI.2 shows the round twin image to be one inverted sky, so that separate galaxies need the broken family's sheets. Two consequences follow. The continuum of observed redshifts cannot come from traversal number and must come from $y_0$ and the profile varying across classes; and the comb of §XIV.8 *may* be washed out by that variation — a possibility, since neither the distribution of $y_0$ nor that of the islands is derived. For a component of height $a_0$ within a width $w$ of each meridian the estimate is $\eta_\Xi\approx2\sqrt\pi\,\epsilon y_0a_0w/\ell$, and the Hubble number $H_0\ell/c\approx4\times10^{-6}$ at $\ell = 16$ kpc asks $\epsilon y_0a_0w/\ell\approx10^{-6}$. The relation of $\eta_\Xi$ to $D_{\rm app}$ is untouched, and the recurrence-age bound itself carries over from Part XIV unchanged. The repair also creates a pressure point of its own, entered in §XIV.8 as the finite-width test: with exactly twin-paired returns not accumulating, large observed redshift must arise within a single class, four to five orders of magnitude above the local calibration and outside the small-$\epsilon$ regime in which the formula is proved.

## XV.4 The action and the empirical firewall [S/O]

The target is no longer Einstein–Hilbert plus a readout but

$$S_{\rm deep} = S_{\rm geom}[\bar\nabla,K,h]+S_{\rm matter}+S_{\rm EM}+\cdots,\qquad K\to-K\ \text{a symmetry}$$

in which matter and single-history propagation see only the even combination $\bar\nabla$, while recurrent two-history objects probe the odd structure through ${\rm Hol}_{\rm rel}(\Xi)$ — and, if the descent of §XV.7 holds, $K$ is not an independent field at all but the analytic continuation of the Hopf connection, with the exchange symmetry being $\rho_E$. The empirical requirement is then brutal and clean: one event producing both electromagnetic and gravitational radiation must show

$$\eta_{\rm EM} = \eta_{\rm GW}$$

for the same canonical class, modulo emission delays, with matched path multiplicities — amplitudes and polarizations free to differ, the class not. The real sky anchors the first half of this **[T]**: the binary neutron-star merger GW170817 and its gamma-ray burst arrived within seconds after some forty megaparsecs, bounding any difference in propagation speed at the $10^{-15}$ level — exactly the shared-cone, no-privileging constraint. It does not yet test the second half: a gravitational waveform carries the mass–redshift degeneracy, so GW170817 measured no gravitational-wave spectral redshift to set against its host's, and its "standard siren" distance agreeing with the host's redshift, $z\approx0.01$, is *consistent with* the shared-redshift proposal rather than evidence for it. The stronger claim awaits measurements that break that degeneracy.

## XV.5 The deeper construction, deferred [S/O]

If neither $B_{\rm rep}$ nor $B_{\rm grav}$ may be the fundamental home of a history — the first houses light, the second gravity, and the principle forbids privileging either — the common home lies before they separate: in $\mathcal M$, or beneath it. The candidate is an incidence construction, two four-dimensional prospect spaces over a common three-dimensional incidence space,

$$\mathcal M = X_+\times_CX_- = \{(x_+,x_-):\pi_+(x_+) = \pi_-(x_-)\},\qquad 4+4-3 = 5$$

which produces the five-dimensional middle space by dimension count: a point of $\mathcal M$ would be an agreement between one $+$ prospect and one $-$ prospect over common relational data — the author's intersecting-leaves intuition as a fiber product rather than two foliations. Twistor theory's double fibrations are the precedent for spacetime as incidence data between deeper null structures, and the chiral $SL(2,\mathbb C)$ sectors of Part I are the obvious candidates for $X_\pm$. This is deliberately not adopted: it would be a *second theorem about* $\mathcal M$, to be proved compatible with $\mathcal M = E/U(1)_H$, not a replacement axiom, and it comes only after the bi-connection has earned its place.

## XV.6 One thing, two representations: the doubled transport [T/D/S/O]

*The author's larger ambition: that the two Born paths a photon always requires and the two connections of §XV.1 are manifestations of one representation-independent structure — one the gravitational analogue of what the other is for quantum particles — with a gauge-like freedom to offload the constraints that define a system's degrees of freedom from the quantum pair onto the Weyl decomposition and back. There is a standard doubled formalism with precisely the algebraic pattern needed for this identification.*

**The closed-time-path dictionary [T].** Quantum probabilities are double path integrals over a closed-time-path contour: a forward branch $\gamma_+$ weighted by $e^{iS[\gamma_+]/\hbar}$ and a backward branch $\gamma_-$ weighted by $e^{-iS[\gamma_-]/\hbar}$, required to close at the final time. Exchanging the branches is complex conjugation. In the Keldysh basis every doubled quantity splits into a *classical* mean and a *quantum* difference, $\varphi_{\rm cl} = \tfrac12(\varphi_++\varphi_-)$ and $\varphi_q = \tfrac12(\varphi_+-\varphi_-)$; the exchange fixes the mean and flips the difference; single-time observables see only the classical component; and the interference between branches is carried by the difference of their actions, $e^{i(S_+-S_-)/\hbar}$ — in the eikonal limit the circulation $\oint k$ of the phase form around the two-leg loop. Set beside §XV.1, line by line:

| closed-time-path | bi-connection |
|:--|:--|
| the doubled object: forward and backward branches — two *histories*, one connection | the doubled object: two *transports* around one loop, $\nabla^\pm$ |
| the classical component, the mean | $\bar\nabla = \nabla^{\rm LC}$, what matter and clocks see |
| the quantum component, the difference | $K = \tfrac12(\nabla^+-\nabla^-)$, what only pairs see |
| branch exchange, complex conjugation | $K\to-K$ |
| the contour closing at the final time | the loop $C_\Xi$ closing at the observer |
| the interference factor $e^{i(S_+-S_-)/\hbar} = e^{i\oint k}$: one form, two paths | the relative holonomy $e^{-2\oint A}$: two connections, one loop — both loop integrals, both exchange-even |
| diagonal histories see no interference | single histories see no relative holonomy |

The same mean-and-difference algebra, with the same exchange, appears in both; but the objects doubled differ — histories in one, transports in the other — and the closed-time-path branches are the doubling that computes probabilities, not by that fact the two separated stationary geodesics at a conjugate point. What is earned is that *the same mathematics of doubling appears in both*; that the two Born paths and the two connections *are one thing* remains a conjecture **[S]**, whose sharpest form §XV.7 states. What the doubling does carry is the representation independence the author asked for: the doubled structure belongs to transports, not to spin, and spin one, spin two and the metric itself are its representations. The doubling makes the adopted principle representation-independent; it does not derive it — that light and gravitational waves share one canonical cone remains the structural postulate of §XV.0.

**One complex holonomy [D].** The two legs carry a single complex relative exponent,

$$H_{\rm rel}(\Xi) = \exp\Big(\tfrac{i}{\hbar}\oint_{C_\Xi}k\;-\;2\oint_{C_\Xi}A\Big)\;\in\;\mathbb C^\times = U(1)\times\mathbb R_+$$

whose compact part is the interference phase — chromatic, read by quantum detectors as fringes, the object the static-wall no-go of §XIV.4 showed cannot be the Hubble redshift — and whose noncompact part is the dilation — achromatic, read as scale. This is a *combined* $\mathbb C^\times$-valued relative factor, and the claim that quantum phase and gravitational scale are the two parts of *one* holonomy is what §XV.7 sets out to prove — it does so for the Hopf phase and the scale, leaving the identification of the eikonal phase $\oint k$ with the Hopf phase as the spin–phase locking of Part IV seen from the optics. This is the third appearance of the same $\mathbb C^\times$: as $w = e^{-\mu+i\chi}$ in the charge branch, where the phase is the meridian angle and the modulus the chiral weight rapidity (§X.0); as Berry's phase with a size in §IX.1, where $F_H$ says how far around the fiber a loop goes and $L_H$ how far that is; and now as phase and scale of the two-leg loop. Whether these are one $\mathbb C^\times$ or three is the branch's deepest open question, and it is stated as such **[S/O]**.

**The offloading theorem [T/D].** The author's gauge flexibility is exact. If a map $\phi$ carries one leg onto the other, fixing the endpoints to first order, transporting along $\gamma_-$ with $\nabla$ equals transporting along $\gamma_+$ with the pulled-back connection $\phi^*\nabla$; so the loop holonomy of one connection around two paths *is* the relative holonomy of two connections along one path: **two paths with one connection equals one path with two connections.** When $\phi$ is a conformal deformation, $\phi^*h = e^{2\sigma}h$, the pulled-back Levi-Civita connection is exactly a Weyl-type connection with $A = d\sigma$ — checked symbolically — so deforming the legs generates precisely the bi-connection's form. But $d\sigma$ is exact, and its holonomy around every loop is trivial: **what can be offloaded into a deformation of the legs carries no redshift.** The part of a relative connection that cannot be so offloaded is, locally, its curvature $F_A$; globally a flat but non-exact connection could still carry holonomy on a non-simply-connected domain. In this architecture that loophole is closed for the scale part: $B_{\rm rep} = S^3$ is simply connected, $H^1(\mathbb{RP}^3;\mathbb R) = 0$ because $\pi_1 = \mathbb Z_2$ is torsion, and $H^1(S^4\setminus\Gamma;\mathbb R)\cong\widetilde H_2(\Gamma;\mathbb R) = 0$ for the meridian graph by Alexander duality, so every flat $\mathbb R_+$-connection is exact and the obstruction to offloading is the curvature alone **[T]**. The phase part is different, and rightly so: $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z_2,U(1)) = \mathbb Z_2$ is the sign monodromy of §XIII.9, and $H^2(S^4\setminus\Gamma)\cong\widetilde H_1(\Gamma)$ is where the Hopf classes live. And that curvature is the obstruction that sources the candidate dilation. This is the coboundary no-go of §XIV.4 in connection language, and it fixes the abstraction level the author proposed: the freedom is real, and the physics is exactly its obstruction.

**The exchange as the twin [S/O].** The pair's exchange involution may be one the theory already has. On the arena the twin map carries each half of a great circle through $p$ and $-p$ onto the other half, so the two arcs of one great circle are a twin-exchanged pair, and on the gravity slice one closed geodesic through the source is already "two legs" **[V]**. If the exchange is $\tau$, then $A$ is twin-odd, $\tau^*A = -A$, the flux through any twin-symmetric surface vanishes, and classes come in opposite-sign pairs under $\tau$ — either the sign rule §XV.3 asked for or its obstacle. That is a calculation, not yet done.

**Status [D].** Standard: the closed-time-path doubling and the Keldysh basis; the naturality of parallel transport under pull-back; the conformal transformation of the Levi-Civita connection. Derived: the dictionary; the complex relative holonomy and its split into phase and scale; that offloading is exact and its obstruction is the curvature, the global loophole being closed for the scale part. Conjectured: that the three $\mathbb C^\times$'s are one — two of them now are, the Hopf phase and the scale (§XV.7); that the exchange is the real structure, established for conjugate lifts, with the twin's role on the legs still open. Open: the dynamics that make the doubling of the *connection* physical rather than a rewriting — the same open item as §XV.4.

## XV.7 The complex connection, constructed [T/V/D/C]

*The question was why $A$ exists and what fixes $F_A$ from geometry the theory already has. The answer is analytic continuation, and it leaves no independent redshift field.*

**Construction [T/V].** On a complexification of the real locus $E$, with holomorphic coordinates $z = x+iy$ and $J\partial_x = \partial_y$, let $\mathcal A_H$ be real-analytic in compatible analytic trivializations — a smooth connection on an analytic manifold need not extend, and in the round arena everything is analytic — and let $\mathcal A_{\mathbb C}$ be a holomorphic $\mathbb C^\times$-connection, a $(1,0)$-form $a_j(z)\,dz^j$ with holomorphic coefficients. The compact real structure on $\mathbb C^\times$ — the one whose fixed set is $U(1)$ — is $w\mapsto1/\bar w$, acting on the Lie algebra as $-\mu+i\chi\mapsto+\mu+i\chi$: it flips the modulus and fixes the phase **[V]**. So the exchange $K\to-K$ of §XV.1 is $\rho_E$ acting on the $\mathbb C^\times$ part. Equivariance, $\rho_E^*\mathcal A_{\mathbb C} = -\overline{\mathcal A_{\mathbb C} }$, forces $a_j = i\,b_j$ with $b_j$ real on the real locus, and holomorphy then makes $b_j(z)$ the unique analytic continuation of $b_j(x)$:

$$\mathcal A_{\mathbb C} = i\,b_j(z)\,dz^j,\qquad \text{on }E:\quad \mathcal A_H = b_j\,dx^j\ \text{(the Hopf connection)},\qquad A = -b_j\,dy^j$$

Three consequences, checked symbolically **[V]**: $A|_{TE} = 0$ — the firewall, single real histories see only $U(1)_H$ and the metric; $A(Jw) = -\mathcal A_H(w)$ for real $w$ — along an imaginary displacement the scale connection *is* the Hopf connection along the corresponding real direction; and, by Cauchy–Riemann, the curvatures obey

$$F_A(v,Jw) = -F_H(v,w),\qquad F_A\big|_{TE\wedge TE} = 0,\qquad F_H(v,Jw) = 0\qquad(v,w\in TE)$$

Tested on the local Hopf model of §II.2, $b_\phi = \tfrac12(1-\cos\theta)$: $F_H(\partial_\theta,\partial_\phi) = \tfrac12\sin\theta$ and $F_A(\partial_\theta,J\partial_\phi) = -\tfrac12\sin\theta$, exactly **[V]**. **The Hopf connection analytically determines its own noncompact partner, uniquely.** So $A$ introduces no new independent field — but $F_A$ is determined by the *full* $\mathcal A_H$, not by its Chern class alone: Structure III deliberately leaves the connection's local, unsourced deformations unassigned, and it is exactly one of those, the mixed meridian component, that the redshift needs.

**The decisive component on the local model [V].** On $E_{\rm loc} = \mathbb R_t\times S^1_\theta\times\mathbb C^2$ with the Hopf action on $\mathbb C^2$, the quotient $\mathcal M_{\rm loc} = \mathbb R_t\times S^1_\theta\times\mathbb R^3$ has the meridian at $x = 0$ and $\partial_{\rm mer} = \partial_\theta$. A connection on the regular stratum with the right Chern class is the monopole on $\mathbb R^3$ — curvature $x\cdot dS/2r^3$, flux $2\pi$ on every linking sphere — plus a meridian component $a_\theta\,d\theta$, a time component $a_t\,dt$, and horizontal deformations $\delta A_i\,dx^i$ that the class does not fix; its mixed curvature is $F_{\theta i} = \partial_\theta A_i-\partial_ia_\theta$ in general, which in a *meridian-invariant gauge*, $\partial_\theta A_i = 0$ — an ansatz the static local model admits and which is stated here rather than assumed silently — reduces to $\bar F_H(\partial_\theta,k) = -k\cdot\nabla a_\theta$ **[V]**. The component $a_\theta$ is gauge-dependent; the mixed curvature is not, and it is the observable. The product connection gives zero; a constant $a_\theta$ is a flat Hopf-phase monodromy around the meridian and gives zero too; nothing in topology or smoothness forces $a_\theta$, which may be finite at the meridian. The component is an unsourced deformation — though not a symmetry-forbidden one, because the meridian carries an orientation from the sign of $c_1$ — and what it does to a class is worked out in §XV.3: a moment that vanishes for like parities and is symmetry-allowed, not guaranteed, for opposite ones, with the parity law itself following from twin equivariance.

**The scale character of conjugate lifts [V/D].** For a history $\widetilde\gamma_+ = \gamma+iY$ and its conjugate $\widetilde\gamma_- = \rho_E\widetilde\gamma_+$, equivariance gives ${\rm Hol}(\widetilde\gamma_-) = 1/\overline{ {\rm Hol}(\widetilde\gamma_+)}$, so the phases cancel in ${\rm Hol}(\widetilde\gamma_-)^{-1}{\rm Hol}(\widetilde\gamma_+)$ and the scales double: the mean of the two transports is the Berry phase a single history sees, their difference the scale only a pair sees — the mean-and-difference algebra of §XV.6 realized by one connection and one involution. Stokes over the complex ribbon then gives §XV.3's variation formula, $\eta_\Xi = 2\epsilon\int\bar F_H(Y,k)\,ds+O(\epsilon^3)$.

**What is conditional [C].** The construction is local and standard — a real-analytic $U(1)$-connection extends uniquely to a holomorphic $\mathbb C^\times$-connection on a neighbourhood of the real locus — and in the round arena everything is analytic. What remains conditional is the *descent*: that the scale part of this continuation on the complex shell lift is the $A$ of the middle-space bi-connection, and that the conjugate lifts of §XV.3 are its holomorphic null curves. Until that is proved, every statement above is **[D, conditional on the descent]**, and the firewall with it.

**What remains [O].** The **twin-equivariant mixed-curvature theorem**: determine from the bundle-form metric or the action whether $f_\Xi = \bar F_H(N,k)$ obeys a forced sign law, the acceptance criterion being $\int\sin(s/\ell)f_\Xi\,ds>0$ for every admissible externally rendered class with the self-return kernel of §XV.3 preserved — the parity law is derived, the sign is not (item 3 of §XI.2); the canonical imaginary width $y_0$ (item (f) of Part X); finite existence of the complex family once lumps split the degeneracy; the descent; whether the eikonal phase $\oint k$ of §XV.6 is the Hopf phase, which is the spin–phase locking of Part IV seen from the optics; and the frequency readout of a length homothety. The chain to the sky is still $\mathcal A_H\to\bar F_H(\partial_{\rm mer},k)\to y_0\to\eta_\Xi\to\omega_{\rm obs}/\omega_{\rm em}\to D(z)$, and nothing here has deduced Hubble phenomenology; what has changed is the question, from which field to invent to whether the existing Hopf connection has the mixed geometry required.

## XV.8 Status and program [D]

| grade | contents |
|:--|:--|
| **standard mathematics and physics** | geometric optics of Maxwell and of high-frequency gravitational waves on null geodesics; their lensing and caustic behaviour; spin-dependent corrections beyond leading order; Weyl connections and the second-clock effect; Stokes; the multi-messenger constraints from GW170817 |
| **adopted by decision** | the bi-connection $(\nabla^+,\nabla^-)$ with exchange symmetry on the middle-space geometry, and the Universal Null-History Principle at leading eikonal order |
| **verified here** | the pair's mean is Levi-Civita; all three connections share every unparametrized null geodesic; the squared-norm transport $e^{\mp2\int A}$, lengths scaling as $e^{\mp\int A}$; the full relative transport lies in $CSp(4,\mathbb R)$ and only its scale character is the homothety $e^{-2\int_\Sigma F_A}$; it is not the Jacobi squeeze, the two being the $\mathbb R_+$ and $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ factors of $GL^+(2,\mathbb R)$; monopole-type flux through a ribbon vanishes, and the enclosed flux is first order in the pair's width; the relative holonomy's definition, with the factor two from comparing the two connections around one loop; the exchange $K\to-K$ as the compact real structure acting on the $\mathbb C^\times$ stabilizer; the global offloading loophole closed for the scale part |
| **constructed here** | the holomorphic $\rho_E$-equivariant $\mathbb C^\times$-connection as the unique analytic continuation of the Hopf connection; $A\vert _{TE} = 0$ and $A(Jw) = -\mathcal A_H(w)$; the bridge $F_A(v,Jw) = -F_H(v,w)$, exact on the Hopf model; the conjugate-lift scale character as a Hopf flux at first order, $\eta_\Xi = 2\epsilon\int\bar F_H(Y,k)\,ds+O(\epsilon^3)$, the residual verified to scale as $\epsilon^3$; the imaginary displacement the first-order Jacobi field of a complex variation, so conjugacy is the necessary infinitesimal gate, with the finite family explicit in the round arena; on the local model $\bar F_H(\partial_{\rm mer},k) = -k\cdot\nabla a_{\rm mer}$, and a class's scale character is $2\epsilon y_0\int\sin(s/\ell)\,\bar F_H(N,k)\,ds$, zero for a component even between source and twin and symmetry-allowed for an odd one; under twin equivariance of the connection the mixed curvature obeys the paired law $f_d(s) = f_{-d}(\pi\ell-s)$, with the weighted moment equal for paired members; with the imaginary direction along the global normal the round smooth self-return, and every exactly twin-paired continuation, lies in the scale kernel |
| **derived here** | the reciprocal-branch problem dissolves once redshift is carried by the homothety; redshift becomes a property of finite classes; the flux's continuous variation can wash out the comb; the recurrence-age bound survives for cumulative readings; the firewall itself, from $\rho_E$-equivariance — the scale part vanishes on real directions and lives on imaginary displacements; hence the real Jacobi ribbon carries no scale flux and the redshift candidate moves to the complex ribbon between conjugate lifts — all conditional on the descent of §XV.7 |
| **open** | a sign rule from the substrate — reduced to the weighted moment $\int\sin(s/\ell)\bar F_H(N,k)\,ds>0$ on admissible classes, with pointwise positivity the sufficient stronger target, neither fixed by twin equivariance; persistence of the self-return kernel under the perturbed return map; the visual kernel, distinct from the scale kernel; the finite-width regime — whether one class family can span $\eta$ from $10^{-6}$ to order one (§XIV.8); the determination of the full Hopf connection $\mathcal A_H$, above all its mixed meridian component, and hence the magnitude of $\eta$; universality across islands; the frequency readout from a length homothety; the fiber-product construction and its compatibility with $E/U(1)_H$; the derivation of both field sectors from one null structure; the descent of the complex connection from the shell lift to the middle-space pair; the canonical width $y_0$; the profile of $a_{\rm mer}$; finite existence of the complex family with lumps; the eikonal phase as the Hopf phase |

**The program, in order.** State the principle — done. Put the pair on the middle-space geometry and demand shared null curves, exchange symmetry and Levi-Civita in the mean — done. Compute its relative holonomy around the conjugate pair and compare with §XIV.9 — done: complementary, not identical. What Part XV has established is a geometrically derived *candidate source* of dilation, not a derived redshift law: $\mathcal A_H\to\mathcal A_{\mathbb C}\to F_A(v,Jw) = -F_H(v,w)\to\eta_\Xi'(0)$ is becoming derived, while $\eta_\Xi\to\omega_{\rm obs}/\omega_{\rm em}$ and $\eta_\Xi\leftrightarrow D_{\rm app}$ are open. Part XV is frozen conceptually: no further mechanism is to be added, and the next phase is calculation rather than architecture. The missing links are five — the field equation for $f_\Xi$, the canonical width $y_0$, the finite-width regime, $\eta\to\omega$, and $\eta\leftrightarrow D_{\rm app}$ — and their order is the field equation first, the finite-width dynamic-range test of §XIV.8 immediately after. Next, then, and before any return-map numerics: the field equation for the mixed Hopf curvature — the twin-equivariant mixed-curvature theorem of §XV.7, from the bundle-form condition or the action of item 3 of §XI.2; then the canonical imaginary width $y_0$ and the descent; then $\eta_\Xi$ on the islands of a return map and the constancy of $\eta/D_{\rm app}$; only then the fiber-product form of $\mathcal M$ and both field sectors from one null structure; and last, whether the homothety acts on frequency with $\omega_{\rm obs} = e^{-\eta_\Xi}\omega_{\rm em}$.
